========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me, I really appreciate it. 

Um... I guess I'm here because I've been having a little trouble with my eating habits lately. It's probably not a huge deal, but... well, most nights when my wife goes to bed, I end up in the kitchen. I just kind of start snacking, and then I end up eating way past a normal plate. 

Like, if we have leftovers from an event or something—fried chicken, mac and cheese, sweet potatoes, rolls, whatever is in there—I'll just keep eating. Sometimes I don't even realize it until a whole tray or, you know, half the fridge is gone. I guess I just get a bit carried away. I mean, a lot of guys my age get late-night cravings, right? 

Am I explaining that okay? Is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?

Well... I guess you could say that. I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally out of my mind or anything, but... yeah. It's sort of like, once I take that very first bite, it almost feels like the decision has already been made for me.

Sometimes I don't even realize it until a whole tray or, you know, half the fridge is gone. I guess I just get a bit carried away. I mean, a lot of guys my age get late-night cravings, right? \n\nAm I explaining that okay? Is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess you could say that. I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally out of my mind or anything, but... yeah. It's sort of like, once I take that very first bite, it almost feels like the decision has already been made for me. \n\nIt's actually a little embarrassing to admit to you, to be honest. But even while I’m standing there in the kitchen telling myself, 'George, stop now, you don't need this,' my hands just kind of... keep going. I'll just keep fixing another plate or opening another container from the fridge. I know I should stop, but I just keep doing it. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange... does that make sense to you? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Um... control. Well, I mean, I guess I'm the one putting the food in my mouth, so technically I have some control, right? It's not like someone is forcing me. 

But... honestly? No. I don't really feel like I'm in control at all. It's like I'm on autopilot, or like a switch flips and I just can't turn it off until I'm so full I can't breathe. I hate admitting that, because I like to think I'm a pretty disciplined guy in other areas of my life. But in those moments... it just feels like the food is in charge, not me.

I just kind of start snacking, and then I end up eating way past a normal plate. \n\nLike, if we have leftovers from an event or something—fried chicken, mac and cheese, sweet potatoes, rolls, whatever is in there—I'll just keep eating. Sometimes I don't even realize it until a whole tray or, you know, half the fridge is gone. I guess I just get a bit carried away. I mean, a lot of guys my age get late-night cravings, right? \n\nAm I explaining that okay? Is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you start during these late-night snacking episodes?\n\nPatient: Well... I guess you could say that. I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally out of my mind or anything, but... yeah. It's sort of like, once I take that very first bite, it almost feels like the decision has already been made for me. \n\nIt's actually a little embarrassing to admit to you, to be honest. But even while I’m standing there in the kitchen telling myself, 'George, stop now, you don't need this,' my hands just kind of... keep going. I'll just keep fixing another plate or opening another container from the fridge. I know I should stop, but I just keep doing it. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange... does that make sense to you? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I don't want to make too big a deal out of it, because I know everyone overeats sometimes, but... yeah. It hits me pretty hard. \n\nI just feel... sick with myself, honestly. I try to be this guy that everybody sees as, you know, put-together, organized, and strong. But then I'm just sitting there in the kitchen, feeling so ashamed and... guilty. And just completely disgusted that I let it happen all over again. \n\nI'm sorry, I don't mean to sound so negative or dramatic. Is that... is that a normal way to feel about something like this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... control. Well, I mean, I guess I'm the one putting the food in my mouth, so technically I have some control, right? It's not like someone is forcing me. \n\nBut... honestly? No. I don't really feel like I'm in control at all. It's like I'm on autopilot, or like a switch flips and I just can't turn it off until I'm so full I can't breathe. I hate admitting that, because I like to think I'm a pretty disciplined guy in other areas of my life. But in those moments... it just feels like the food is in charge, not me. \n\nI really hope I'm not coming across as completely helpless or anything. I know I need to do better. Does that answer your question? I'm trying to be as honest as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I wouldn't say I'm completely rushing like it's a race or anything. I don't want to make it sound like I have zero manners. But... yeah, I guess I do eat pretty fast when it happens. \n\nIt's actually kind of sad, because I used to really take pride in cooking and enjoying my meals, you know? But during those late-night times... I eat so fast I barely even taste it. Sometimes I catch myself just standing right there at the counter, swallowing big bites before I've even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm really trying to work on slowing down. I hope that's the kind of detail you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding too... out of control.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an every single night kind of thing, I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart. But... yeah, it's been happening pretty often. \n\nFor months now, honestly, I can't really think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And... well, most weeks, it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed, so I don't bother her or make her worry. \n\nI know that's probably more than it should be, and I'm really sorry if that sounds bad. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well... I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I'm completely out of control, like I'm some kind of monster or anything. I can usually stop myself from doing things I shouldn't do in other parts of my life. I'm a pretty disciplined guy, normally.

But... with the eating late at night... it's a little harder. I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel a bit like... once I take that first bite, the decision is sort of already made for me? 

It's embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I'm literally standing there telling myself, 'George, stop now, you don't need this,' but... my hands just keep fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. It's like my brain knows I should stop, but I just can't seem to make myself do it in that moment.

I know that sounds really weak, and I'm so sorry if that's disappointing to hear. I really am trying to get a handle on it. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to be as helpful as I can.

I don't want to make too big a deal out of it, because I know everyone overeats sometimes, but... yeah. It hits me pretty hard. \n\nI just feel... sick with myself, honestly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... control. I hate admitting that, because I like to think I'm a pretty disciplined guy in other areas of my life. I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding too... out of control.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an every single night kind of thing, I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart. I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. Is that... does that help give you an idea of the timeline? I don't want to make too big a deal out of it, because I know everyone overeats sometimes, but... yeah. It hits me pretty hard. \n\nI just feel... sick with myself, honestly. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... control. No. I don't really feel like I'm in control at all. It's like I'm on autopilot, or like a switch flips and I just can't turn it off until I'm so full I can't breathe. I hate admitting that, because I like to think I'm a pretty disciplined guy in other areas of my life. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
How many nights? Um... well, I try to keep it to a minimum, obviously. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm just completely off the rails every single day. 

But... if I'm being completely honest with you—and I want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate information so you can help me—for months now, I can't really think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And, um... most weeks, it's actually been several nights. Usually right after my wife goes to bed and the house is quiet. 

I hope that doesn't sound too terrible? I know it's a lot, and I'm really not proud of it. I promise I'm ready to do whatever you think is best to get it under control. I just want to be totally transparent with you. Is that... is that a pretty common frequency for people dealing with this kind of thing, or is that really bad?

I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty or ashamed after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, yes. I don't want to make too big a deal out of it, because I know everyone overeats sometimes, but... yeah. It hits me pretty hard. \n\nI just feel... sick with myself, honestly. I try to be this guy that everybody sees as, you know, put-together, organized, and strong. But then I'm just sitting there in the kitchen, feeling so ashamed and... guilty. And just completely disgusted that I let it happen all over again. \n\nI'm sorry, I don't mean to sound so negative or dramatic. Is that... is that a normal way to feel about something like this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what you're eating during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... control. Well, I mean, I guess I'm the one putting the food in my mouth, so technically I have some control, right? It's not like someone is forcing me. \n\nBut... honestly? No. I don't really feel like I'm in control at all. It's like I'm on autopilot, or like a switch flips and I just can't turn it off until I'm so full I can't breathe. I hate admitting that, because I like to think I'm a pretty disciplined guy in other areas of my life. But in those moments... it just feels like the food is in charge, not me. \n\nI really hope I'm not coming across as completely helpless or anything. I know I need to do better. Does that answer your question? I'm trying to be as honest as I can for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating quickly during these late-night episodes?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I wouldn't say I'm completely rushing like it's a race or anything. I don't want to make it sound like I have zero manners. But... yeah, I guess I do eat pretty fast when it happens. \n\nIt's actually kind of sad, because I used to really take pride in cooking and enjoying my meals, you know? But during those late-night times... I eat so fast I barely even taste it. Sometimes I catch myself just standing right there at the counter, swallowing big bites before I've even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm really trying to work on slowing down. I hope that's the kind of detail you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding too... out of control.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an every single night kind of thing, I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart. But... yeah, it's been happening pretty often. \n\nFor months now, honestly, I can't really think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And... well, most weeks, it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed, so I don't bother her or make her worry. \n\nI know that's probably more than it should be, and I'm really sorry if that sounds bad. I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. Is that... does that help give you an idea of the timeline? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can do your job.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these late-night episodes because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... stressed or upset? Well, I mean, I guess everyone gets a little stressed sometimes, right? Life is just busy. But I wouldn't say I'm, like, a super anxious person or anything. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude most of the time. \n\nBut... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I don't really notice feeling particularly upset before it happens. It's more just... I'm up late, the house is quiet, and I just start eating. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm not trying to be difficult or avoid the question, I just... I haven't really made a connection between feeling bad and the eating. Is it okay if I don't have a better answer for that one? I can try to pay more attention to it if you think I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started, even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I'm completely out of control, like I'm some kind of monster or anything. I can usually stop myself from doing things I shouldn't do in other parts of my life. I'm a pretty disciplined guy, normally.\n\nBut... with the eating late at night... it's a little harder. I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel a bit like... once I take that first bite, the decision is sort of already made for me? \n\nIt's embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I'm literally standing there telling myself, 'George, stop now, you don't need this,' but... my hands just keep fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. It's like my brain knows I should stop, but I just can't seem to make myself do it in that moment.\n\nI know that sounds really weak, and I'm so sorry if that's disappointing to hear. I really am trying to get a handle on it. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are usually triggered by feeling bored, rather than stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Bored? Um... well, I guess that's possible. I mean, I'm not working right now, so my days are pretty open, and I try to keep myself busy, I really do. I don't want you to think I just sit around all day doing nothing. \n\nBut at night, when everything's quiet and my wife is asleep... yeah, maybe it is just boredom. There's not much else going on, you know? It's just me and the TV, and I guess the food is just... there. \n\nI'm not totally sure, honestly. I haven't really pinpointed one specific thing that sets it off. I hope that's an okay answer? I can definitely try to keep a closer eye on whether I'm feeling bored right before it happens, if you think that would be helpful for us to figure this out. I just want to give you whatever information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant weight gain since these late-night eating episodes started happening?\n\nPatient: Weight gain? Um... well, I mean, maybe a little bit? I try not to weigh myself too often, honestly. I don't want to get obsessed with the numbers or anything. I think my clothes still fit mostly the same, maybe just a tiny bit snugger, but nothing crazy. \n\nI definitely wouldn't say it's, like, a huge amount. I try to walk around the neighborhood when the weather's nice, so I'm not totally inactive. \n\nI haven't really noticed a huge difference, but... maybe I'm just not paying close enough attention? I hope it's alright that I don't have an exact number for you. I could start weighing myself if you think that's something I need to be tracking for you? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing here.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these late-night episodes because you're feeling emotional, such as sad, angry, or lonely?\n\nPatient: Emotional? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm a super emotional guy in general. I try to keep things pretty even-keeled, you know? \n\nI don't really feel sad or angry when it happens. Like I said before, it's mostly just quiet at night. My wife is asleep, the house is still... maybe lonely is a strong word? It's just... solitary, I guess. \n\nI'm not sitting there crying into a bowl of ice cream or anything like that, I promise. I try to be pretty positive most of the time. I just... I don't really know exactly what I'm feeling right before it starts. Is it bad that I don't have a better answer for that? I can try to pay more attention to my mood if you think that's what's causing all this. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... well, I mean... I guess nobody feels great after eating too much, right? It's just a normal thing to feel a little bad about it. \n\nBut... yeah, I do. I feel pretty bad afterward. It's just... I like to think of myself as a strong guy, you know? Someone who has it together. And people see me that way, like I'm organized and in control. So when I do this, it's just... I feel sick with myself, honestly. \n\nI sit there and I just feel so ashamed and disgusted that I let it happen again. It's embarrassing, really. I try so hard to be good during the day, and then... During the day? Um... well, I try my absolute best to keep things completely normal while the sun's up. I mean, my wife is around, and I'm trying to be productive and keep busy, so I really make an effort to eat like a regular person. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around eating all day long.

Not often! Just... occasionally. But really, it's almost always at night when the house is quiet. 

I hope it's okay that it's mostly a nighttime thing? I'm really trying to keep it from spilling over into the daytime. Does that make sense, or is it usually an all-day problem for people? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.

I mean, I wouldn't say I'm completely rushing like it's a race or anything. I don't want to make it sound like I have zero manners. But... yeah, I guess I do eat pretty fast when it happens. \n\nIt's actually kind of sad, because I used to really take pride in cooking and enjoying my meals, you know? But during those late-night times... I eat so fast I barely even taste it. Sometimes I catch myself just standing right there at the counter, swallowing big bites before I've even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm really trying to work on slowing down. I hope that's the kind of detail you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding too... out of control.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an every single night kind of thing, I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart. But... yeah, it's been happening pretty often. \n\nFor months now, honestly, I can't really think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And... well, most weeks, it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed, so I don't bother her or make her worry. \n\nI know that's probably more than it should be, and I'm really sorry if that sounds bad. I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. Is that... does that help give you an idea of the timeline? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can do your job.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these late-night episodes because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... stressed or upset? Well, I mean, I guess everyone gets a little stressed sometimes, right? Life is just busy. But I wouldn't say I'm, like, a super anxious person or anything. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude most of the time. \n\nBut... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I don't really notice feeling particularly upset before it happens. It's more just... I'm up late, the house is quiet, and I just start eating. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm not trying to be difficult or avoid the question, I just... I haven't really made a connection between feeling bad and the eating. Is it okay if I don't have a better answer for that one? I can try to pay more attention to it if you think I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started, even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I'm completely out of control, like I'm some kind of monster or anything. I can usually stop myself from doing things I shouldn't do in other parts of my life. I'm a pretty disciplined guy, normally.\n\nBut... with the eating late at night... it's a little harder. I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel a bit like... once I take that first bite, the decision is sort of already made for me? \n\nIt's embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I'm literally standing there telling myself, 'George, stop now, you don't need this,' but... my hands just keep fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. It's like my brain knows I should stop, but I just can't seem to make myself do it in that moment.\n\nI know that sounds really weak, and I'm so sorry if that's disappointing to hear. I really am trying to get a handle on it. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are usually triggered by feeling bored, rather than stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Bored? I don't want you to think I just sit around all day doing nothing. \n\nBut at night, when everything's quiet and my wife is asleep... yeah, maybe it is just boredom. There's not much else going on, you know? It's just me and the TV, and I guess the food is just... there. \n\nI'm not totally sure, honestly. I try to walk around the neighborhood when the weather's nice, so I'm not totally inactive. \n\nI haven't really noticed a huge difference, but... maybe I'm just not paying close enough attention? But... yeah, I guess I do eat pretty fast when it happens. \n\nIt's actually kind of sad, because I used to really take pride in cooking and enjoying my meals, you know? But during those late-night times... I eat so fast I barely even taste it. Sometimes I catch myself just standing right there at the counter, swallowing big bites before I've even finished chewing the last one. \n\nI know that probably sounds terrible, and I'm really trying to work on slowing down. I hope that's the kind of detail you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm giving you a clear picture without sounding too... out of control.\n\nDoctor: How often do these late-night eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not like an every single night kind of thing, I don't want you to think I'm completely falling apart. But... yeah, it's been happening pretty often. \n\nFor months now, honestly, I can't really think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And... well, most weeks, it's been several nights. Usually after my wife goes to bed, so I don't bother her or make her worry. \n\nI know that's probably more than it should be, and I'm really sorry if that sounds bad. I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. Is that... does that help give you an idea of the timeline? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information so you can do your job.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these late-night episodes because you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: Um... stressed or upset? Well, I mean, I guess everyone gets a little stressed sometimes, right? Life is just busy. But I wouldn't say I'm, like, a super anxious person or anything. I try to keep a pretty positive attitude most of the time. \n\nBut... I don't know, maybe? I haven't really thought about it like that. I guess I don't really notice feeling particularly upset before it happens. It's more just... I'm up late, the house is quiet, and I just start eating. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm not trying to be difficult or avoid the question, I just... I haven't really made a connection between feeling bad and the eating. Is it okay if I don't have a better answer for that one? I can try to pay more attention to it if you think I should.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started, even if you want to?\n\nPatient: Well... I mean, I definitely wouldn't say I'm completely out of control, like I'm some kind of monster or anything. I can usually stop myself from doing things I shouldn't do in other parts of my life. I'm a pretty disciplined guy, normally.\n\nBut... with the eating late at night... it's a little harder. I guess, if I'm being completely honest, it does feel a bit like... once I take that first bite, the decision is sort of already made for me? \n\nIt's embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I'm literally standing there telling myself, 'George, stop now, you don't need this,' but... my hands just keep fixing another plate or opening another container anyway. It's like my brain knows I should stop, but I just can't seem to make myself do it in that moment.\n\nI know that sounds really weak, and I'm so sorry if that's disappointing to hear. I really am trying to get a handle on it. Does that... does that answer your question okay? I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are usually triggered by feeling bored, rather than stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Bored? Um... well, I guess that's possible. I mean, I'm not working right now, so my days are pretty open, and I try to keep myself busy, I really do. I don't want you to think I just sit around all day doing nothing. \n\nBut at night, when everything's quiet and my wife is asleep... yeah, maybe it is just boredom. There's not much else going on, you know? It's just me and the TV, and I guess the food is just... there. \n\nI'm not totally sure, honestly. I haven't really pinpointed one specific thing that sets it off. I hope that's an okay answer? I can definitely try to keep a closer eye on whether I'm feeling bored right before it happens, if you think that would be helpful for us to figure this out. I just want to give you whatever information you need.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant weight gain since these late-night eating episodes started happening?\n\nPatient: Weight gain? Um... well, I mean, maybe a little bit? I try not to weigh myself too often, honestly. I don't want to get obsessed with the numbers or anything. I think my clothes still fit mostly the same, maybe just a tiny bit snugger, but nothing crazy. \n\nI definitely wouldn't say it's, like, a huge amount. I try to walk around the neighborhood when the weather's nice, so I'm not totally inactive. \n\nI haven't really noticed a huge difference, but... maybe I'm just not paying close enough attention? I hope it's alright that I don't have an exact number for you. I could start weighing myself if you think that's something I need to be tracking for you? I just want to make sure I'm doing what I'm supposed to be doing here.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat during these late-night episodes because you're feeling emotional, such as sad, angry, or lonely?\n\nPatient: Emotional? Um... I don't think so. I mean, I wouldn't say I'm a super emotional guy in general. I try to keep things pretty even-keeled, you know? \n\nI don't really feel sad or angry when it happens. Like I said before, it's mostly just quiet at night. My wife is asleep, the house is still... maybe lonely is a strong word? It's just... solitary, I guess. \n\nI'm not sitting there crying into a bowl of ice cream or anything like that, I promise. I try to be pretty positive most of the time. I just... I don't really know exactly what I'm feeling right before it starts. Is it bad that I don't have a better answer for that? I can try to pay more attention to my mood if you think that's what's causing all this. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel guilty after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Guilty? Um... well, I mean... I guess nobody feels great after eating too much, right? It's just a normal thing to feel a little bad about it. \n\nBut... yeah, I do. I feel pretty bad afterward. It's just... I like to think of myself as a strong guy, you know? Someone who has it together. And people see me that way, like I'm organized and in control. So when I do this, it's just... I feel sick with myself, honestly. \n\nI sit there and I just feel so ashamed and disgusted that I let it happen again. It's embarrassing, really. I try so hard to be good during the day, and then... I don't know. I hope it doesn't make you think less of me, admitting that. I know I should be stronger than this. I'm really trying to figure it out, I promise. Is it normal to feel that disgusted with yourself? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the full picture, even if it's not very flattering.\n\nDoctor: How many nights a week would you say you have one of these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: How many nights? Um... well, I try to keep it to a minimum, obviously. I really don't want to make it sound like I'm just completely off the rails every single day. \n\nBut... if I'm being completely honest with you—and I want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate information so you can help me—for months now, I can't really think of a single week where it didn't happen at least once. And, um... most weeks, it's actually been several nights. Usually right after my wife goes to bed and the house is quiet. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too terrible? I know it's a lot, and I'm really not proud of it. I promise I'm ready to do whatever you think is best to get it under control. I just want to be totally transparent with you. Is that... is that a pretty common frequency for people dealing with this kind of thing, or is that really bad?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're losing control over your eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Losing control? Um... well, I wouldn't say I completely lose my mind or anything like that. I'm still, you know, aware of what's happening. I don't want you to think I'm totally out of touch with reality when I'm in the kitchen.\n\nBut... I guess if I'm being really honest, and I want to make sure I'm giving you the right answer... yeah, it does feel a bit like that. It's just... once I take that very first bite, it's like a switch flips. It feels like the decision has already been made for me, somehow. \n\nI'll literally be standing there, in my own head, telling myself, \"George, stop now. You don't need this.\" But my hands... they just keep moving. They keep fixing another plate, or opening another container, even while I'm telling myself to quit. It's really frustrating because I *want* to stop, I really do. I hope that makes sense? I'm trying so hard to be disciplined, but it's like my body just won't listen to my brain in those moments. Does that sound like what you mean by losing control? I just want to be sure I'm explaining it the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel uncomfortably full after these late-night eating episodes?\n\nPatient: Uncomfortably full? Um... well, I wouldn't really say that. I mean, I try really hard not to let it get to that point, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just... gorging myself until I'm sick or something. \n\nTo be honest, I haven't really noticed feeling like that. Maybe I just kind of tune out my body when it's happening? I'm usually so focused on the... the guilt of it all right afterward, that I don't really pay attention to how my stomach actually feels. I hope that's an okay answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need. Is it normal that I'm not really sure about the physical fullness part?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have these eating episodes during the day, or are they only at night?\n\nPatient: During the day? Um... well, I try my absolute best to keep things completely normal while the sun's up. I mean, my wife is around, and I'm trying to be productive and keep busy, so I really make an effort to eat like a regular person. I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around eating all day long. \n\nBut... I mean, I guess if I'm being perfectly honest—and I want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture—there have been a few times when she's gone out to run errands and... well, it has happened in the afternoon. Not often! Just... occasionally. But really, it's almost always at night when the house is quiet. \n\nI hope it's okay that it's mostly a nighttime thing? I'm really trying to keep it from spilling over into the daytime. Does that make sense, or is it usually an all-day problem for people? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your late-night eating episodes are triggered by feeling emotional?\n\nPatient: Emotional? Um... well, I don't really think so. I mean, I try pretty hard to keep my emotions in check, you know? I don't want you to think I'm just... sitting in the dark crying and eating or anything like that. I try to be a pretty level-headed guy. \n\nTo be honest, I haven't really noticed any specific feeling that sets it off. Sometimes it happens even on a really good day, or just a completely normal, boring Tuesday. It's more of just... an urge that pops up, rather than me feeling sad or stressed out about something specific. \n\nI hope it's okay if I say no to that one? I know a lot of people probably do it because they're upset, so I hope I'm not messing up your evaluation by saying I don't really see an emotional connection. Is it still normal if it just happens without a bad mood triggering it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers so you can do your job.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Hungry? Um... well, I want to make sure I'm giving you the most accurate answer here, but... I don't want to sound like I'm just completely out of control or anything.
